The original Justin.TV was a website created by Justin Kahn, Emmett Scheer, and Michael Siebel, and Kyle Voigt in 2007 to allow anyone to broadcast video online. Justin.TV user accounts were called channels, like those on YouTube. Okay. Users were encouraged to broadcast a wide variety of user-generated live video content called broadcasts. That's awesome. The company was an internet startup based in San Francisco with seed funding from Paul Graham of Y Combinator. The original Justin.TV was a single channel featuring founder Justin Kahn, who broadcast his life 24-7. Oh, I think I've heard of this. And popularized the term lifecasting. In 2007, Justin.TV stopped broadcasting, and Justin.TV relaunched into its latter form as a network of thousands of channels. Okay. Users were permitted to broadcast to an unlimited number of people for free, and watching broadcasts did not require user registration. Broadcasts that were considered to contain potentially offensive content were available only to registered users over the age of 18. Broadcasts containing defamation, pornography, or copyright violations, or organizing criminal conduct, were prohibited by Justin.TV's terms of service. A worm is a program that autonomously copies itself from machine to machine, whether or not you want it to. Here is an AI worm. A baby worm. A proof of concept.
